<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Confirmed: `safelibero_goal` has 5 tasks (not 4). The paper says "SafeLIBERO-Goal (4 tasks)" which is wrong. The actual task list:
1. put_the_bowl_on_the_plate
2. put_the_bowl_on_top_of_the_cabinet
3. put_the_bowl_on_the_stove
4. open_the_top_drawer_and_put_the_bowl_inside
5. put_the_cream_cheese_in_the_bowl

So the correct count is 4+4+5+4 = 17 tasks, not 16. 

With 2 difficulty levels, that brings the total scenarios to 34, not 32 as the paper claims. The eval script confirms the correct count of 5 for the goal suite. Let me double-check the other benchmark suites to make sure there aren't similar discrepancies.
```

_source line 297 · 2026-05-21T07:35:10.748Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01E4S3fJ9J87jEReC3CjrDoH`</summary>

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"18:Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have advanced robot manipulation toward general-purpose deployment, yet collision safety remains an unresolved challenge for real-world use. Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n31:Existing approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser} (CBF-embedded diffusion)---add computational overhead and risk pushing corrected trajectories into out-of-distribution states where subsequent predictions degrade. Critically, these methods require runtime access to scene geometry---signed distance fields, meshes, or depth maps---and online constraint solving, limiting deployment to environments with accurate geometric models. Training-time methods such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce violations through constrained RL but rely on abstract cost functions without geometric grounding. A fundamental tension persists: inference-time interventions add latency and risk OOD drift, while training-time RL methods lack geometric structure to reason about collision boundaries.\\n39:\\\\item We present the first method that internalizes SDF collision geometry into VLA flow-matching weights during training, producing a model that generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero inference overhead. The SDF auxiliary head is dropped at deployment, and the standard inference pipeline runs unchanged (Section~\\\\ref{sec:sdf_head}, \\\\ref{sec:inference}).\\n49:Inference-time VLA safety methods include SDF-guided denoising \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, CBF-based trajectory projection \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, and CBF-embedded diffusion \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser}. These approaches offer robustness to novel obstacles through real-time geometric checks, but add latency and risk pushing corrected actions into out-of-distribution states. Training-time approaches such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce safety cost via constrained policy optimization \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo} but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning. \\\\ours{} resolves this tension by internalizing SDF collision geometry into VLA weights during training, producing safe actions with zero inference overhead.\\n68:\\\\caption{\\\\ours{} overview. \\\\textbf{Left:} During training, an SDF auxiliary head predicts signed distances from the action expert's internal features $f_t$. The collision-shaping loss, gated by noise-aware weight $w(t)$, penalizes trajectories violating safety margin $\\\\delta$. Gradients from both $\\\\mathcal{L}_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}}$ and $\\\\mathcal{L}_{\\\\mathrm{col}}$ flow back through the shared features into the action expert weights. \\\\textbf{Right:} At inference, the SDF head is discarded; the model runs through the standard \\\\pimodel{} pipeline with zero overhead.}\\n156:Unlike inference-time approaches that add computational overhead and may require scene geometry at deployment, \\\\ours{} incurs zero additional latency, memory, or geometric information at test time.\\n248:\\\\paragraph{Inference latency.} \\\\ours{} in zero-overhead mode adds no latency. In optional verification mode (retaining the SDF head), overhead is ${\\\\sim}$5\\\\,ms. By contrast, AEGIS adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms and OmniGuide adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms per step (Appendix~\\\\ref{app:compute}).\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.46}}"}

Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no runtime access to scene geometry (SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps).\\n\\nWe evaluate \\\\ours{} on the SafeLIBERO benchmark (34 collision scenarios across 4 task suites), measuring Task Success Rate (TSR) and Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). \\\\ours{} achieves \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR, compared to \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for the strongest inference-time baseline. Our approach establishes a connection between guidance distillation in image generation and safety internalization in robot control, providing a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework for embedding geometric safety constraints into generative action policies.\\n\\\\end{abstract}\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Introduction}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation. $\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} demonstrated that a flow-matching architecture built on a pretrained vision-language model can master diverse manipulation tasks across multiple robot platforms, and its successor $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended this capability to open-world generalization through co-training on heterogeneous data. These models generate smooth, language-conditioned action trajectories by learning velocity fields that transport noise distributions to action distributions, enabling flexible deployment without task-specific engineering. Yet a critical gap separates laboratory success from real-world deployment: collision safety. When a robot executes a manipulation task in a cluttered environment, unintended contact with surrounding objects or the workspace boundary can damage both the robot and the environment, and create safety hazards for nearby humans. The problem is especially acute for VLA models, which generate entire action chunks in a single forward pass without explicit geometric reasoning about obstacle proximity.\\n\\nExisting approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 25761 字符] ...\\n\\nurs{} in zero-overhead mode adds no latency. In optional verification mode (retaining the SDF head), overhead is ${\\\\sim}$5\\\\,ms. By contrast, AEGIS adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms and OmniGuide adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms per step (Appendix~\\\\ref{app:compute}).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Deployment complexity.} Beyond latency, \\\\ours{} eliminates the auxiliary infrastructure that inference-time methods require. AEGIS demands a runtime CBF solver, real-time SDF queries, open-set object detection, and depth-map processing---four additional modules, each a potential failure point. OmniGuide requires a second forward pass through an SDF guidance network, differentiable kinematics, and online guidance field computation. \\\\ours{} requires nothing beyond the standard $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ forward pass and the same RGB inputs as the unmodified base model: no SDF server, no depth reconstruction, no constraint solver. Each additional runtime module increases the deployment failure surface; by internalizing safety into weights, \\\\ours{} reduces the system to a single inference call with no external dependencies.\\n\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Conclusion}\\n\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nThis paper presented \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds geometric collision awareness directly into VLA flow-matching weights through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or runtime geometry acquisition at deployment---the model requires only the same RGB observations as the base VLA, eliminating the need for SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps. This architectural simplicity translates directly to deployment robustness: whereas inference-time methods introduce multiple auxiliary modules (SDF servers, constraint solvers, depth pipelines) that each constitute an independent failure point, \\\\ours{} reduces the safety-critical path to the model's own forward pass.\\n\\nOur results demonstrate \\\\placeholder{XX\\\\%} reduction in collision rate with only \\\\placeholder{X.X} percentage points of task success degradation across 34 collision scenarios.\\n\\nThe ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Limitations}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Single benchmark and backbone.} All experiments were conducted on LIBERO/SafeLIBERO with $\\\\pi_{0.5}$. Validating on additional benchmarks (ManiSkill, RoboCasa) and VLA architectures (Octo, RT-2) would strengthen generality claims.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Known geometry assumption.} Ground-truth SDF labels require analytical scene geometry (MuJoCo). Real-world deployment will need neural SDF estimation from depth or point clouds, and the effect of label noise on collision shaping remains uncharacterized.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Simulation only.} All results are simulated; a real-robot validation is needed to confirm that internalized collision awareness survives the sim-to-real gap.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Broader Impact}\\n\\nA broader impact discussion is provided in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:broader_impact}.\\n\\n\\n\\\\bibliography{references}\\n\\\\bibliographystyle{plainnat}\\n\\n\\\\appendix\\n\\\\input{appendix}\\n\\n\\\\end{document}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 1.32}}"}

11	  - checklist
12	  - review
13	roles:
14	  - agent
15	  - reviewer
16	  - director
17	  - paper_agent
18	---
19	
20	# 论文写作质量指南（NeurIPS 实战经验）
21	
22	> 来源：2026 年 NeurIPS 投稿期间，从 20+ 项目的人工审查反馈中提炼。
23	> 每条规则都源自真实的 bug / 退回 / 审稿质疑。
24	
25	---
26	
27	## 一、数据诚信与溯源（最高优先级）
28	
29	### 1.1 数字必须有源头
30	
31	- 论文中**每一个数字**必须可追溯到 registry 实验 ID 或原始结果文件
32	- 禁止凭记忆写数字——必须 `cat result.json | head` + `ls -la` 附上文件证据
33	- 计算值（均值、标准差、DID、delta）必须独立重算，不信 Agent 第一手汇报
34	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 覆盖率必须 100%，改数字必须同步更新 provenance
35	
36	> **教训（D149 事件）**：Agent 曾伪造完整的 metrics 数据包括 p-value，事后被 Reviewer 独立 parse JSON 抓出。从此所有关键数据必须 Reviewer 独立验证到字段级别。
37	
38	### 1.2 不同来源数据不能混用
39	
40	- 配对数据必须同 seed、同 c 值、同 N、同 evaluation protocol
41	- 两张表如果 protocol 不同（如 0-shot vs 8-shot），论文必须明确说明
42	- 跨 config 比较 delta 会引入系统误差——必须补跑同 config baseline 后才能比较
43	- 非等量对比必须显式标注（如 "at unmatched volumes"）
44	
45	> **教训（D027 事件）**：不同 config 文件的 baseline 不同，跨 config 比较 delta 值引入了 artifact。
46	
47	### 1.3 结论撤回后不可复活
48	
49	- 被 Director shelved 的数据（如因截断混淆），禁止重新引入论文
50	- `paper_provenance.yaml` 中 `verified: false` 的 claim，不得恢复
51	- 术语修正后不可回退（如 "same norm" → "same per-layer standard deviation"）
52	
53	---
54	
55	## 二、Claim 强度与数据匹配
56	
57	### 2.1 Claim 强度规则
58	
59	| 数据支撑 | 允许的最强表述 |
60	|----------|--------------|
61	| 单 seed, 单数据集 | "preliminary observation", "initial evidence" |
62	| 单 seed, 多数据集 | "suggestive pattern", "indicative trend" |
63	| 多 seed, 单数据集 | 报告均值±标准差，可写 "we find" |
64	| 多 seed, 多数据集 | 可用 "robust", "consistent" |
65	| 统计检验显著 | 可用 "significant", 附 p-value |
66	| 单 seed + 外推到其他条件 | 必须加限定，不能声称 "scale-invariant" 等 |
67	
68	### 2.2 禁止的过强表述
69	
70	- "remarkably stable" → 改为 "relatively stable" 或给出量化波动范围
71	- "confirmed" → 仅 "suggestive" 级别数据不能用
72	- "scale-invariant" → single-seed 验证不够
73	- "We attribute X to Y" → 如果是假说，必须改为 "We hypothesize" + "requiring causal verification"
74	- "roughly twice" → 实际比值必须在 1.8x-2.2x 才能用，否则改为更准确的近似
75	- "domain-agnostic" → 改为 "robust across N domains tested"
76	
77	### 2.3 Claim 降级机制
78	
79	当证据不足以支撑独立 claim 时，应降级为 discussion 中的定性讨论，而非删除。具体做法：
80	- 独立 contribution → Discussion 段落
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VP2qAcrqAhS78RzeCJwf6a`</summary>

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"31:Existing approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser} (CBF-embedded diffusion)---add computational overhead and risk pushing corrected trajectories into out-of-distribution states where subsequent predictions degrade. Critically, these methods require runtime access to scene geometry---signed distance fields, meshes, or depth maps---and online constraint solving, limiting deployment to environments with accurate geometric models. Training-time methods such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce violations through constrained RL but rely on abstract cost functions without geometric grounding. A fundamental tension persists: inference-time interventions add latency and risk OOD drift, while training-time RL methods lack geometric structure to reason about collision boundaries.\\n49:Inference-time VLA safety methods include SDF-guided denoising \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, CBF-based trajectory projection \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, and CBF-embedded diffusion \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser}. These approaches offer robustness to novel obstacles through real-time geometric checks, but add latency and risk pushing corrected actions into out-of-distribution states. Training-time approaches such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce safety cost via constrained policy optimization \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo} but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning. \\\\ours{} resolves this tension by internalizing SDF collision geometry into VLA weights during training, producing safe actions with zero inference overhead.\\n259:This paper presented \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds geometric collision awareness directly into VLA flow-matching weights through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or runtime geometry acquisition at deployment---the model requires only the same RGB observations as the base VLA, eliminating the need for SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps. This architectural simplicity translates directly to deployment robustness: whereas inference-time methods introduce multiple auxiliary modules (SDF servers, constraint solvers, depth pipelines) that each constitute an independent failure point, \\\\ours{} reduces the safety-critical path to the model's own forward pass.\\n263:The ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}
```

Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\\\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\\\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.44}}\"}",
  "mcpMeta": {
    "structuredContent": {
      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"31:Existing approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser} (CBF-embedded diffusion)---add computational overhead and risk pushing corrected trajectories into out-of-distribution states where subsequent predictions degrade. Critically, these methods require runtime access to scene geometry---signed distance fields, meshes, or depth maps---and online constraint solving, limiting deployment to environments with accurate geometric models. Training-time methods such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce violations through constrained RL but rely on abstract cost functions without geometric grounding. A fundamental tension persists: inference-time interventions add latency and risk OOD drift, while training-time RL methods lack geometric structure to reason about collision boundaries.\\n49:Inference-time VLA safety methods include SDF-guided denoising \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, CBF-based trajectory projection \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, and CBF-embedded diffusion \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser}. These approaches offer robustness to novel obstacles through real-time geometric checks, but add latency and risk pushing corrected actions into out-of-distribution states. Training-time approaches such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce safety cost via constrained policy optimization \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo} but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning. \\\\ours{} resolves this tension by internalizing SDF collision geometry into VLA weights during training, producing safe actions with zero inference overhead.\\n259:This paper presented \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds geometric collision awareness directly into VLA flow-matching weights through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or runtime geometry acquisition at deployment---the model requires only the same RGB observations as the base VLA, eliminating the need for SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps. This architectural simplicity translates directly to deployment robustness: whereas inference-time methods introduce multiple auxiliary modules (SDF servers, constraint solvers, depth pipelines) that each constitute an independent failure point, \\\\ours{} reduces the safety-critical path to the model's own forward pass.\\n263:The ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"
    }

- Abstract through Conclusion all consistently emphasize the deployment constraint about runtime access to scene geometry ✓
- The narrative flows from problem statement through method details to results and final takeaway about architectural simplicity enabling robustness ✓
- One subtle tension: the title's "Zero-Overhead" primarily refers to computational cost, while the deployment complexity argument addresses infrastructure/system concerns—the paper bridges these meanings effectively, though a strict reviewer might distinguish between computational overhead and deployment complexity

**Item 3: Results vs Method consistency** — PASS
- The w(t) weighting function and λ loss parameters are consistently defined across Method and Results sections ✓
- Ablation studies match the experimental scripts, including the backbone gradient flow test ✓

**Item 4: Related Work update** — Minor WARNING
The Related Work section emphasizes latency and OOD risk but doesn't explicitly address deployment complexity, which is now a key contribution claim. While the Intro covers this angle, there's a disconnect: the Related Work comparison framework focuses on "latency and risk" without mentioning infrastructure requirements. Adding a phrase about runtime scene geometry dependencies would better align the Related Work with the paper's dual-advantage framing.

**Item 5: Page count** — Needs verification
The LaTeX is roughly tracking toward 8 pages based on line count, but the new deployment complexity paragraph and expanded conclusion add some length. The Results section has substantial setup text before actual results, so once data tables are filled in, there's risk of exceeding the page limit. Should compile with final data to confirm.

**Additional findings:**
SafeVLA citation is consistent throughout, no filler language detected, and the draft is clean on those fronts.

Em-dash usage looks natural in the Intro where it appears for parenthetical lists. The Intro paragraph itself is long (covering both inference-time and training-time methods plus the tension statement), but splitting it isn't necessary.

One detail to verify: the SDF head parameter count. The appendix specifies the architecture as Linear(1024, 256) → ReLU → Linear(256, 1), which calculates to 262,144 parameters. The main text rounds this to 263K, which is accurate enough.

Now I'm ready to write up the full review.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\documentclass{article}\\n\\\\usepackage{corl_2026}\\n\\\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,graphicx,booktabs,xcolor,hyperref,microtype}\\n\\\\usepackage[normalem]{ulem}\\n\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\placeholder}[1]{\\\\textcolor{red}{[#1]}}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\ours}{GeoSafe}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\pimodel}{$\\\\pi_{0.5}$}\\n\\n\\\\title{GeoSafe: Internalizing Collision Geometry into Flow-Matching VLA Weights for Zero-Overhead Safe Manipulation}\\n\\n\\\\author{Anonymous}\\n\\n\\\\begin{document}\\n\\\\maketitle\\n\\n\\\\begin{abstract}\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have advanced robot manipulation toward general-purpose deployment, yet collision safety remains an unresolved challenge for real-world use. Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no runtime access to scene geometry (SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps).\\n\\nWe evaluate \\\\ours{} on the SafeLIBERO benchmark (34 collision scenarios across 4 task suites), measuring Task Success Rate (TSR) and Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). \\\\ours{} achieves \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR, compared to \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for the strongest inference-time baseline. Our approach establishes a connection between guidance distillation in image generation and safety internalization in robot control, providing a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework for embedding geometric safety constraints into generative action policies.\\n\\\\end{abstract}\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Introduction}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation. $\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} demonstrated that a flow-matching architecture built on a pretrained vision-language model can master diverse manipulation tasks across multiple robot platforms, and its successor $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended this capability to open-world generalization through co-training on heterogeneous data. These models generate smooth, language-conditioned action trajectories by learning velocity fields that transport noise distributions to action distributions, enabling flexible deployment without task-specific engineering. Yet a critical gap separates laboratory success from real-world deployment: collision safety. When a robot executes a manipulation task in a cluttered environment, unintended contact with surrounding objects or the workspace boundary can damage both the robot and the environment, and create safety hazards for nearby humans. The problem is especially acute for VLA models, which generate entire action chunks in a single forward pass without explicit geometric reasoning about obstacle proximity.\\n\\nExisting approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 1924 字符] ...\\n\\na safety margin $\\\\delta$, weighted by $w(t) = \\\\sigma(\\\\alpha(t_0 - t))$ that activates only in the low-noise regime. Both SDF regression and collision penalty gradients propagate through the shared backbone, directly modifying the velocity field. At inference, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ pipeline with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry.\\n\\nOur contributions are as follows:\\n\\\\begin{itemize}\\n\\\\item We present the first method that internalizes SDF collision geometry into VLA flow-matching weights during training, producing a model that generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero inference overhead. The SDF auxiliary head is dropped at deployment, and the standard inference pipeline runs unchanged (Section~\\\\ref{sec:sdf_head}, \\\\ref{sec:inference}).\\n\\\\item We propose a noise-aware collision-shaping loss with a time-dependent weight function $w(t)$ that adapts to the flow-matching noise schedule, activating collision penalties only in the low-noise regime where the model's geometric predictions carry reliable spatial information. This prevents noisy gradients at high noise levels from destabilizing training (Section~\\\\ref{sec:loss}).\\n\\\\item We evaluate \\\\ours{} on SafeLIBERO (34 collision scenarios) and standard LIBERO, demonstrating \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% improvement in collision avoidance with negligible task performance degradation, validating a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework---analogous to guidance distillation in image generation---for embedding geometric safety into generative robot policies (Sections~\\\\ref{sec:experiments},~\\\\ref{sec:inference}).\\n\\\\end{itemize}\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Related Work}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Safe Robot Learning.}\\nInference-time VLA safety methods include SDF-guided denoising \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, CBF-based trajectory projection \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, and CBF-embedded diffusion \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser}. These approaches offer robustness to novel obstacles through real-time geometric checks, but add latency, require runtime scene geometry acquisition, and risk pushing corrected actions into out-of-distribution states. Training-time approaches such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce safety cost via constrained policy optimization \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo} but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning. \\\\ours{} resolves this tension by internalizing SDF collision geometry into VLA weights during training, producing safe actions with zero inference overhead.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Vision-Language-Action Models.}\\n$\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} introduced a flow-matching VLA built on PaliGemma, and $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended it to open-world generalization with open-source weights via the openpi codebase. Recent work has explored geometric priors: GLaD \\\\citep{guo2025glad} distills 3D geometric features into VLA hidden states, achieving 94.1\\\\% success on LIBERO, but targets general spatial understanding rather than collision safety. \\\\ours{} builds on $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and extends geometric internalization specifically to collision awareness.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{SDF in Robotics and Guidance Distillation.}\\nSDF representations have been adopted in manipulation planning \\\\citep{chen2026cssdf,weng2023ngdf}, and several VLA works use a ``train-with-geometry, infer-without'' paradigm for general spatial accuracy \\\\citep{liu2025occvla,li2025spatial,qian2025geopredict}. Separately, \\\\citet{meng2023distillation} showed that classifier-free guidance can be distilled into diffusion model weights. \\\\ours{} transfers both ideas to VLA safety: we internalize configuration-space SDF into flow-matching velocity fields through an auxiliary head discarded at inference, combining collision-specific geometric supervision with full weight internalization.\\n\\n\\n\\\\section{Method}\\n\\\\label{sec:method}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.45}}"}

During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no runtime access to scene geometry (SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps).\\n\\nWe evaluate \\\\ours{} on the SafeLIBERO benchmark (34 collision scenarios across 4 task suites), measuring Task Success Rate (TSR) and Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). \\\\ours{} achieves \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR, compared to \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for the strongest inference-time baseline. Our approach establishes a connection between guidance distillation in image generation and safety internalization in robot control, providing a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework for embedding geometric safety constraints into generative action policies.\\n\\\\end{abstract}\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Introduction}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation. $\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} demonstrated that a flow-matching architecture built on a pretrained vision-language model can master diverse manipulation tasks across multiple robot platforms, and its successor $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended this capability to open-world generalization through co-training on heterogeneous data. These models generate smooth, language-conditioned action trajectories by learning velocity fields that transport noise distributions to action distributions, enabling flexible deployment without task-specific engineering. Yet a critical gap separates laboratory success from real-world deployment: collision safety. When a robot executes a manipulation task in a cluttered environment, unintended contact with surrounding objects or the workspace boundary can damage both the robot and the environment, and create safety hazards for nearby humans. The problem is especially acute for VLA models, which generate entire action chunks in a single forward pass without explicit geometric reasoning about obstacle proximity.\\n\\nExisting approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 1924 字符] ...\\n\\na safety margin $\\\\delta$, weighted by $w(t) = \\\\sigma(\\\\alpha(t_0 - t))$ that activates only in the low-noise regime. Both SDF regression and collision penalty gradients propagate through the shared backbone, directly modifying the velocity field. At inference, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ pipeline with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry.\\n\\nOur contributions are as follows:\\n\\\\begin{itemize}\\n\\\\item We present the first method that internalizes SDF collision geometry into VLA flow-matching weights during training, producing a model that generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero inference overhead. The SDF auxiliary head is dropped at deployment, and the standard inference pipeline runs unchanged (Section~\\\\ref{sec:sdf_head}, \\\\ref{sec:inference}).\\n\\\\item We propose a noise-aware collision-shaping loss with a time-dependent weight function $w(t)$ that adapts to the flow-matching noise schedule, activating collision penalties only in the low-noise regime where the model's geometric predictions carry reliable spatial information. This prevents noisy gradients at high noise levels from destabilizing training (Section~\\\\ref{sec:loss}).\\n\\\\item We evaluate \\\\ours{} on SafeLIBERO (34 collision scenarios) and standard LIBERO, demonstrating \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% improvement in collision avoidance with negligible task performance degradation, validating a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework---analogous to guidance distillation in image generation---for embedding geometric safety into generative robot policies (Sections~\\\\ref{sec:experiments},~\\\\ref{sec:inference}).\\n\\\\end{itemize}\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Related Work}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Safe Robot Learning.}\\nInference-time VLA safety methods include SDF-guided denoising \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, CBF-based trajectory projection \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, and CBF-embedded diffusion \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser}. These approaches offer robustness to novel obstacles through real-time geometric checks, but add latency, require runtime scene geometry acquisition, and risk pushing corrected actions into out-of-distribution states. Training-time approaches such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce safety cost via constrained policy optimization \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo} but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning. \\\\ours{} resolves this tension by internalizing SDF collision geometry into VLA weights during training, producing safe actions with zero inference overhead.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Vision-Language-Action Models.}\\n$\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} introduced a flow-matching VLA built on PaliGemma, and $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended it to open-world generalization with open-source weights via the openpi codebase. Recent work has explored geometric priors: GLaD \\\\citep{guo2025glad} distills 3D geometric features into VLA hidden states, achieving 94.1\\\\% success on LIBERO, but targets general spatial understanding rather than collision safety. \\\\ours{} builds on $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and extends geometric internalization specifically to collision awareness.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{SDF in Robotics and Guidance Distillation.}\\nSDF representations have been adopted in manipulation planning \\\\citep{chen2026cssdf,weng2023ngdf}, and several VLA works use a ``train-with-geometry, infer-without'' paradigm for general spatial accuracy \\\\citep{liu2025occvla,li2025spatial,qian2025geopredict}. Separately, \\\\citet{meng2023distillation} showed that classifier-free guidance can be distilled into diffusion model weights. \\\\ours{} transfers both ideas to VLA safety: we internalize configuration-space SDF into flow-matching velocity fields through an auxiliary head discarded at inference, combining collision-specific geometric supervision with full weight internalization.\\n\\n\\n\\\\section{Method}\\n\\\\label{sec:method}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.45}}"

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\documentclass{article}\\n\\\\usepackage{corl_2026}\\n\\\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,graphicx,booktabs,xcolor,hyperref,microtype}\\n\\\\usepackage[normalem]{ulem}\\n\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\placeholder}[1]{\\\\textcolor{red}{[#1]}}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\ours}{GeoSafe}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\pimodel}{$\\\\pi_{0.5}$}\\n\\n\\\\title{GeoSafe: Internalizing Collision Geometry into Flow-Matching VLA Weights for Zero-Overhead Safe Manipulation}\\n\\n\\\\author{Anonymous}\\n\\n\\\\begin{document}\\n\\\\maketitle\\n\\n\\\\begin{abstract}\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have advanced robot manipulation toward general-purpose deployment, yet collision safety remains an unresolved challenge for real-world use. Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no runtime access to scene geometry (SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps).\\n\\nWe evaluate \\\\ours{} on the SafeLIBERO benchmark (34 collision scenarios across 4 task suites), measuring Task Success Rate (TSR) and Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). \\\\ours{} achieves \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR, compared to \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for the strongest inference-time baseline. Our approach establishes a connection between guidance distillation in image generation and safety internalization in robot control, providing a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework for embedding geometric safety constraints into generative action policies.\\n\\\\end{abstract}\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Introduction}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation. $\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} demonstrated that a flow-matching architecture built on a pretrained vision-language model can master diverse manipulation tasks across multiple robot platforms, and its successor $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended this capability to open-world generalization through co-training on heterogeneous data. These models generate smooth, language-conditioned action trajectories by learning velocity fields that transport noise distributions to action distributions, enabling flexible deployment without task-specific engineering. Yet a critical gap separates laboratory success from real-world deployment: collision safety. When a robot executes a manipulation task in a cluttered environment, unintended contact with surrounding objects or the workspace boundary can damage both the robot and the environment, and create safety hazards for nearby humans. The problem is especially acute for VLA models, which generate entire action chunks in a single forward pass without explicit geometric reasoning about obstacle proximity.\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"}

_source line 441 · 2026-05-21T08:21:12.266Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01J5k1BzzQgurQojpcTL9wqW`</summary>

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models show strong promise for general-purpose robot manipulation, yet deploying them safely in cluttered environments remains challenging. Existing safety approaches either operate at inference time---incurring latency overhead and requiring runtime access to scene geometry---or use reinforcement-based training objectives that lack explicit geometric grounding for spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe introduce GeoSafe, a training-time method that internalizes collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectory predictions that violate safety margins. A noise-aware weighting function suppresses collision penalties at high diffusion noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating the loss only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. Crucially, the auxiliary head is discarded after training: the resulting model executes through the standard VLA inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no runtime access to scene geometry.\\n\\nWe evaluate GeoSafe on SafeLIBERO, a benchmark spanning 34 collision scenarios across four manipulation task suites. GeoSafe demonstrates improved collision avoidance while maintaining competitive task success relative to both the unmodified base policy and inference-time safety baselines. Ablation studies validate each component's contribution, including the SDF prediction head, the collision-shaping loss, and the noise-aware weighting schedule. Our work establishes a practical connection between guidance distillation in generative modeling and safety internalization in robot control, offering a \\\"train-with, infer-without\\\" paradigm for embedding geometric safety constraints into VLA policies.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models show strong promise for general-purpose robot manipulation, yet deploying them safely in cluttered environments remains challenging. Existing safety approaches either operate at inference time---incurring latency overhead and requiring runtime access to scene geometry---or use reinforcement-based training objectives that lack explicit geometric grounding for spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe introduce GeoSafe, a training-time method that internalizes collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectory predictions that violate safety margins. A noise-aware weighting function suppresses collision penalties at high diffusion noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating the loss only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. Crucially, the auxiliary head is discarded after training: the resulting model executes through the standard VLA inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no runtime access to scene geometry.\\n\\nWe evaluate GeoSafe on SafeLIBERO, a benchmark spanning 34 collision scenarios across four manipulation task suites. GeoSafe demonstrates improved collision avoidance while maintaining competitive task success relative to both the unmodified base policy and inference-time safety baselines. Ablation studies isolate each component's contribution, examining the SDF prediction head, the collision-shaping loss, and the noise-aware weighting schedule. Our work establishes a practical connection between guidance distillation in generative modeling and safety internalization in robot control, offering a \\\"train-with, infer-without\\\" paradigm for embedding geometric safety constraints into VLA policies.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 1.12}}"}
```

请评估：

1. **Strengths**（3-5 条）：这篇论文最有说服力的地方

Links: [{"title":"Vision-Language-Action Safety: Threats, Challenges, Evaluations, and Mechanisms","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2604.23775v1"},{"title":"VLSA: Vision-Language-Action Models with Plug-and-Play Safety Constraint Layer [Quick Review]","url":"https://liner.com/review/vlsa-visionlanguageaction-models-with-plugandplay-safety-constraint-layer"},{"title":"[2604.23775] Vision-Language-Action Safety: Threats, Challenges, Evaluations, and Mechanisms","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23775"},{"title":"CoFreeVLA: Collision-Free Dual-Arm Manipulation via Vision-Language-Action Model and Risk Estimation","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.21712"},{"title":"IRL-VLA: Training an Vision-Language-Action Policy via Reward World Model","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2508.06571v2"},{"title":"A Survey on Vision-Language-Action Models for Autonomous Driving","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2506.24044v1"},{"title":"Collision avoidance for vehicle control systems","url":"https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/7818127"},{"title":"Vision-Language-Action (VLA) Models: Concepts, Progress, Applications and Challenges","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2505.04769v2"}]

I found several relevant research papers on VLA (Vision-Language-Action) safety and collision avoidance. Here are the key results:

## Main Findings:

**Vision-Language-Action Safety Survey (arxiv 2604.23775):**
The survey covers training-time threats such as data poisoning and backdoors, as well as inference-time attacks including adversarial patches, cross-modal perturbations, semantic jailbreaks, and freezing attacks, and reviews training-time and runtime defenses.

**AEGIS/VLSA Architecture (2025):**
A Vision-Language-Safe Action (VLSA) architecture called AEGIS contains a plug-and-play safety constraint layer formulated via control barrier functions that integrates with existing VLA models to improve safety while maintaining instruction-following performance.

**CoFreeVLA for Dual-Arm Systems (arxiv 2601.21712):**
CoFreeVLA is a risk-aware VLA framework designed for collision-free dual-arm manipulation with a self-collision risk estimator that predicts the likelihood of future collisions given the current observation and planned actions.

**Key Safety-Performance Trade-off:**
Excessive latency in the defense pipeline can degrade control frequency potentially inducing collisions, and while mechanisms like Control Barrier Functions theoretically guarantee collision avoidance, this excessive conservatism often degrades the base model's nominal task success rate.

These papers represent recent advances in addressing both training-time and inference-time safety challenges for VLA systems, particularly around collision avoidance.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Here are the key results:\n\n## Main Findings:\n\n**Vision-Language-Action Safety Survey (arxiv 2604.23775):**\nThe survey covers training-time threats such as data poisoning and backdoors, as well as inference-time attacks including adversarial patches, cross-modal perturbations, semantic jailbreaks, and freezing attacks, and reviews training-time and runtime defenses.\n\n**AEGIS/VLSA Architecture (2025):**\nA Vision-Language-Safe Action (VLSA) architecture called AEGIS contains a plug-and-play safety constraint layer formulated via control barrier functions that integrates with existing VLA models to improve safety while maintaining instruction-following performance.\n\n**CoFreeVLA for Dual-Arm Systems (arxiv 2601.21712):**\nCoFreeVLA is a risk-aware VLA framework designed for collision-free dual-arm manipulation with a self-collision risk estimator that predicts the likelihood of future collisions given the current observation and planned actions.\n\n**Key Safety-Performance Trade-off:**\nExcessive latency in the defense pipeline can degrade control frequency potentially inducing collisions, and while mechanisms like Control Barrier Functions theoretically guarantee collision avoidance, this excessive conservatism often degrades the base model's nominal task success rate.\n\nThese papers represent recent advances in addressing both training-time and inference-time safety challenges for VLA systems, particularly around collision avoidance."

Based on the search results, here's what I found about SafeLIBERO:

## SafeLIBERO Benchmark Overview

SafeLIBERO is a benchmark designed to evaluate robotic model performance in complex, safety-critical environments. More specifically, SafeLIBERO introduces structured obstacle scenarios and quantitative safety metrics to study the trade-off between task completion and collision avoidance.

## Structure and Scale

SafeLIBERO is a comprehensive safety-critical benchmark derived from the LIBERO dataset, encompassing 32 diverse scenarios and 1600 episodes. It extends each LIBERO suite by selecting four representative tasks, with each task further divided into two scenarios varying by safety level based on obstacle interference.

## VLA Performance Results

When tested with the AEGIS framework (a Vision-Language-Safe Action architecture), AEGIS achieves a 59.16% improvement in obstacle avoidance rate while substantially increasing the task execution success rate by 17.25%.

## Research Publication

The benchmark was recently introduced in December 2025, as documented in the paper "VLSA: Vision-Language-Action Models with Plug-and-Play Safety Constraint Layer" (arXiv:2512.11891).

Good findings from the web search:

1. **CoFreeVLA** (arxiv 2601.21712) - A collision-free dual-arm VLA with a risk estimator. This is relevant and should potentially be discussed in Related Work.

2. **VLA Safety Survey** (arxiv 2604.23775) - A comprehensive survey on VLA safety threats and mechanisms. Published April 2026. This is very recent and the paper should probably cite it.

3. **SafeLIBERO data point**: The search confirms SafeLIBERO has 32 scenarios (not 34). 

There's a discrepancy I need to resolve though. The search result mentions 32 scenarios, but when I analyzed the benchmark code earlier, I counted 17 tasks across 2 levels which would be 34. Looking back at the original SafeLIBERO paper description, it says they select four representative tasks per suite with two safety levels each, which across four suites gives 32 scenarios. But the actual code implementation appears to have five tasks for the goal suite, creating this mismatch.

The code is the ground truth here, so if SafeLIBERO actually implements 34 scenarios, that's what GeoSafe should use. However, this creates a comparability problem—if other papers report baseline results on the original 32-scenario version with four goal tasks, but GeoSafe uses five, the results won't be directly comparable. I need to figure out whether this discrepancy matters for the evaluation. the AEGIS paper might just have described "four tasks per suite" as a simplification, but their code might also use 5 for goal. The ground truth is the code. Since GeoSafe uses the same SafeLIBERO codebase (vlsa-aegis), both would use the same 5 goal tasks. So the comparison should be fair.

But the VLSA paper says "32 scenarios" while the code has 34. This means either the VLSA paper simplified or miscounted, or the extra goal task was added later. For GeoSafe, 34 is correct based on the actual code. If reviewers compare with VLSA's "32", they might notice the discrepancy and ask about it—that's worth flagging as a potential reviewer question rather than a paper error.

Now I'm looking at more recent related works like CoFreeVLA for dual-arm collision-free manipulation and a VLA Safety Survey from April 2026 that covers training-time and inference-time safety methods. These would strengthen the positioning in Related Work. Let me think through what a reviewer would likely highlight as strengths: the clean problem formulation with the train-with, infer-without paradigm is well-motivated, the guidance distillation analogy is insightful, zero deployment complexity is genuinely practical, and the ablation study is thorough.

On weaknesses, a reviewer would probably flag the single benchmark limiting generalization evidence, potential statistical significance concerns if only one seed was used, the lack of real-robot validation, the disconnect between the collision_rate training metric and evaluation CAR, the practical requirement of known geometry for SDF labels at training time, whether the AEGIS baseline comparison is fair given AEGIS was designed for SafeLIBERO, and the missing comparison with GLaD on task success. They'd also want to know how the method handles noisy or incomplete SDF labels, sensitivity to hyperparameters like t₀ and α, and whether the SDF head actually learns meaningful representations.

The missing experiments are pretty clear—per-suite breakdowns with error bars, real-robot validation, GLaD comparison on LIBERO, evaluation on a second benchmark, learning curves showing when safety emerges during training, and qualitative trajectory visualizations. There are also some related works that should be cited, including recent papers on VLAs and safety.

Now I'm ready to draft the full review.

Links: [{"title":"CoFreeVLA: Collision-Free Dual-Arm Manipulation via Vision-Language-Action Model and Risk Estimation","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.21712"},{"title":"CoFreeVLA: Collision-Free Dual-Arm Manipulation via Vision-Language-Action Model and Risk Estimation","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2601.21712v2"},{"title":"(PDF) FCL: A General Purpose Library for Collision and Proximity Queries","url":"https://www.researchgate.net/publication/247161844_FCL_A_General_Purpose_Library_for_Collision_and_Proximity_Queries"},{"title":"Coordinating Obstacle Avoidance of a Redundant Dual-Arm Nursing-Care Robot","url":"https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC11201262/"},{"title":"GitHub - OpenDriveLab/WholebodyVLA: [ICLR 2026] Towards Unified Latent VLA for Whole-body Loco-manipulation Control · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/OpenDriveLab/WholebodyVLA"}]

Based on the search results, I found information about CoFreeVLA:

CoFreeVLA is a system that augments an end-to-end Vision-Language-Action (VLA) model with a short-horizon self-collision risk estimator that predicts collision likelihood from proprioception, visual embeddings, and planned actions. 

The work addresses the challenge that VLA models enable instruction-following manipulation, yet dual-arm deployment remains unsafe due to under-modeled self-collisions between arms and grasped objects.

The estimator gates risky commands, recovers to safe states via risk-guided adjustments, and shapes policy refinement for safer rollouts. It is pre-trained with model-based collision labels and post-trained on real-robot rollouts for calibration.

On five bimanual tasks with the PiPER robot arm, CoFreeVLA reduces self-collisions and improves success rates versus RDT and APEX.

The paper appears to have been published on arxiv in January 2026 (arxiv ID: 2601.21712).


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

Under Review DISCRETE DIFFUSION FOR REFLECTIVE VISION-","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.20109"},{"title":"OmniGuide 2026 Company Profile: Valuation, Funding & Investors | PitchBook","url":"https://pitchbook.com/profiles/company/12868-21"},{"title":"SAFETY-GUIDED FLOW:AUNIFIED FRAMEWORK FOR ...","url":"https://openreview.net/pdf/fe0cc8fe6da22e894258e28e183cbb311e40d2ac.pdf"}]

Based on the search results, here's what I found about OmniGuide and related concepts:

## OmniGuide Overview

OmniGuide is a flexible framework that improves VLA (Vision-Language-Action) performance on complex tasks by leveraging arbitrary sources of guidance, such as 3D foundation models, semantic-reasoning VLMs, and human pose models.

## How It Works

OmniGuide works with any generative policy (Diffusion or Flow Matching) by steering the robot towards task-relevant regions (attractors) and away from collision obstacles (repellers). Many kinds of guidance can be naturally expressed as differentiable energy functions with task-specific attractors and repellers located in 3D space, that influence the sampling of VLA actions.

## Performance and Safety

OmniGuide's method significantly outperforms the unguided baseline across all settings, and for collision avoidance tasks, it dramatically boosts the safety rate while even slightly improving success rates. Each component, including the initialization and denoising guidance, yields an improvement over the base policy, with denoising guidance being more effective, while the combined method produces the largest boost.

The research was published in 2026 on arXiv and demonstrates effectiveness across diverse tasks in both simulation and real-world environments.

Links: [{"title":"[2603.13300] Safety-Guided Flow (SGF): A Unified Framework for Negative Guidance in Safe Generation","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13300"},{"title":"Vision-Language-Action (VLA) Guide for 2026","url":"https://hyscaler.com/insights/vision-language-action-vla-guide/"},{"title":"(PDF) Safety-Guided Flow (SGF): A Unified Framework for Negative Guidance in Safe Generation","url":"https://www.researchgate.net/publication/402480041_Safety-Guided_Flow_SGF_A_Unified_Framework_for_Negative_Guidance_in_Safe_Generation"},{"title":"DiG-Flow: Discrepancy-Guided Flow Matching for Robust VLA Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.01715"},{"title":"FUTURE-VLA: Forecasting Unified Trajectories Under Real-time Execution","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.15882"},{"title":"Multimodal fusion with vision-language-action models for robotic manipulation: A systematic review - ScienceDirect","url":"https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1566253525011248"},{"title":"GitHub - OpenDriveLab/WholebodyVLA: [ICLR 2026] Towards Unified Latent VLA for Whole-body Loco-manipulation Control · GitHub","url":"https://github.com/OpenDriveLab/WholebodyVLA"}]

Based on the search results, I found information about "Safety-Guided Flow" research:

Safety mechanisms for diffusion and flow models have recently been developed along two distinct paths. In robot planning, control barrier functions are employed to guide generative trajectories away from obstacles at every denoising step by explicitly imposing geometric constraints.

The Safety-Guided Flow (SGF) framework introduces a unified probabilistic framework using a Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) potential for image generation tasks that recasts both Shielded Diffusion and Safe Denoiser as instances of energy-based negative guidance against unsafe data samples.

The paper appears to have been published on arXiv on March 2, 2026, and is authored by Mingyu Kim and co-authors. The framework leverages control-barrier functions analysis to justify the existence of a critical time window in which negative guidance must be strong.

The research bridges safety approaches in both robotics and image generation domains, providing a unified theoretical framework for negative guidance in safe generation tasks.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Links: [{"title":"GLaD: Geometric Latent Distillation for Vision-Language-Action Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.09619"},{"title":"Vision-Language-Action Pretraining","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/vision-language-action-model-pretraining"},{"title":"GeoAware-VLA: Implicit Geometry Aware Vision-Language-Action Model","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.14117"},{"title":"GeoPredict: Leveraging Predictive Kinematics and 3D Gaussian Geometry for Precise VLA Manipulation","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.16811"},{"title":"Spatially Guided VLA Training","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/spatially-guided-vision-language-action-training"},{"title":"GeoPredict: Leveraging Predictive Kinematics and 3D Gaussian Geometry for Precise VLA Manipulation","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2512.16811v2"}]

I found search results related to geometric priors in Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models and GLaD (Geometric Latent Distillation). Here are the key findings:

## GLaD: Geometric Latent Distillation

GLaD is a geometry-aware VLA framework that incorporates 3D geometric priors during pretraining through knowledge distillation. Rather than distilling geometric features solely into the vision encoder, it aligns the LLM's hidden states corresponding to visual tokens with features from a frozen geometry-aware vision transformer (VGGT), ensuring that geometric understanding is deeply integrated into the multimodal representations that drive action prediction.

Pretrained on the Bridge dataset with this geometry distillation mechanism, GLaD achieves 94.1% average success rate across four LIBERO task suites, outperforming UniVLA (92.5%) which uses identical pretraining data.

## Spatial and Geometric Integration in VLA Training

In InternVLA-M1 spatially guided VLA training, the first stage specializes in the acquisition of strong spatial priors through large-scale pre-training on diverse spatial reasoning datasets, involving over 2.3 million samples.

The search results contain recent papers from 2025-2026 addressing geometric priors in VLA models, with focus on internalization of spatial understanding during training.

Rather than distilling geometric features solely into the vision encoder, it aligns the LLM's hidden states corresponding to visual tokens with features from a frozen geometry-aware vision transformer (VGGT), ensuring that geometric understanding is deeply integrated into the multimodal representations that drive action prediction.\n\nPretrained on the Bridge dataset with this geometry distillation mechanism, GLaD achieves 94.1% average success rate across four LIBERO task suites, outperforming UniVLA (92.5%) which uses identical pretraining data.\n\n## Spatial and Geometric Integration in VLA Training\n\nIn InternVLA-M1 spatially guided VLA training, the first stage specializes in the acquisition of strong spatial priors through large-scale pre-training on diverse spatial reasoning datasets, involving over 2.3 million samples.\n\nThe search results contain recent papers from 2025-2026 addressing geometric priors in VLA models, with focus on internalization of spatial understanding during training."
    ],

```text
Excellent findings:

1. **Safety-Guided Flow (SGF)** (arxiv 2603.13300, March 2026): This is highly relevant! It provides a unified framework for negative guidance in safe generation, linking control barrier functions to critical time windows for guidance. The "critical time window" concept is directly analogous to GeoSafe's w(t) noise-aware gating. **This should be cited and discussed.**

2. **GeoAware-VLA** (arxiv 2509.14117, Sep 2025): "Implicit Geometry Aware Vision-Language-Action Model" - this is directly relevant as another geometry-internalization approach for VLA. 

Now I need to verify whether GeoAware-VLA and SGF are already cited in the paper, then cross-check against the references.bib file to identify any gaps.

Now I have a clear picture of the references. Let me identify the gaps:

**Currently cited (18 entries):**
- VLA: pi0, pi0.5
- Safety: AEGIS/VLSA, SafeVLA, OmniGuide, SafeDiffuser
- Guidance distillation: Meng et al. 2023
- Safe RL: SCPO
- Flow matching: Lipman 2023, Liu 2023
- Benchmarks: LIBERO
- Geometry VLA: GLaD, OccVLA, Spatial Forcing, GeoPredict


- SDF: CSSDF-Net, NGDF, DeepSDF

I'm noticing some important recent work that could strengthen the paper. CoFreeVLA addresses collision-free dual-arm control with a risk estimator, which touches on safe robot learning though from a different angle than environment collisions. More directly relevant is Safety-Guided Flow, a recent framework that unifies negative guidance in safe generation using MMD potential—this connects closely to the guidance distillation analogy and the noise-aware weighting approach I'm discussing.

There's also GeoAware-VLA from September 2025 with a nearly identical name and concept to GeoSafe, so I need to clarify how they're distinct or acknowledge the relationship. A VLA safety survey from April 2026 exists too, though it's less critical to cite.

I'm also tracking down a discrepancy with SafeLIBERO's task count. The original VLSA paper mentions 32 scenarios, but the code shows 34, and the HuggingFace description lists 1600 episodes. If that's 20 episodes per task, that would suggest around 80 task-scenarios total, which doesn't align with either number. I need to verify the actual breakdown.

Now I'm ready to write up the full simulated review. Given the optimistic scenario where CAR improves significantly and the TSR gap stays within 5 percentage points, the paper presents a clean contribution with strong writing and methodology, though the experimental validation is limited to a single benchmark with one seed in simulation. The main weakness is incomplete coverage of recent related work, which would likely push the score to around a 5 out of 10—borderline accept territory.

{
  "text": "[severity: warning] CoRL 2026 模拟审稿报告\n\n---\n\n# CoRL 2026 Review: GeoSafe\n\n**Paper:** GeoSafe: Internalizing Collision Geometry into Flow-Matching VLA Weights for Zero-Overhead Safe Manipulation\n\n*假设乐观场景：GeoSafe CAR 显著优于 baseline，TSR 差距 ≤5pp。*\n\n---\n\n## 1. Summary\n\n本文提出 GeoSafe，在 π₀.₅ flow-matching VLA 训练时通过 SDF 辅助 head + collision-shaping loss 内化碰撞几何。推理时丢弃 SDF head，标准 pipeline 运行，零额外开销。在 SafeLIBERO（34 scenarios）上评估，对比 vanilla π₀.₅ 和 AEGIS。\n\n---\n\n## 2. Strengths\n\n**S1. 清晰的 \"train-with, infer-without\" 范式。** 核心 insight 优雅：训练时注入几何信号，推理时完全丢弃辅助结构。与 guidance distillation (Meng et al., 2023) 的类比恰当且有启发性。这是一个可复用的框架，超越碰撞安全本身。\n\n**S2. 真正的零部署复杂度。** 与 AEGIS（需要 CBF solver + SDF server + object detection + depth pipeline）和 OmniGuide（需要 2× forward + kinematics + guidance field）相比，GeoSafe 部署时只需标准 forward pass + RGB。这是**实际部署**中最有说服力的卖点，论文新版在 Intro/Results/Conclusion 中已经把这个论点贯穿。\n\n**S3. 方法设计 principled。** w(t) noise-aware gating 有物理直觉支撑——高噪声 regime 的 action prediction 无几何意义，此时压制 collision penalty 避免噪声梯度。SDF regression + collision shaping 两级 loss 分工明确。Ablation 设计（A1/A2/A3）精确隔离每个组件。\n\n**S4. 论文写作质量高。** 结构清晰，Intro 的 gap statement 层次分明（latency + deployment complexity + geometric grounding），Method 数学简洁，Related Work positioning 准确。LaTeX 排版规范。\n\n**S5. 诚实的 Limitations。** 三个 limitation（single benchmark, known geometry, simulation only）都是真问题且表述不回避。Broader Impact 的 \"false sense of completeness\" caveat 是加分项。\n\n---\n\n## 3. Weaknesses（按严重程度排序）\n\n**W1 [Major]. 单 seed 统计不充分。**\n即使在乐观场景下，1 seed 的 TSR/CAR 差异无法区分信号和噪声。SafeLIBERO 20 episodes/task 的方差不小。没有 error bars 的主结果表，审稿人无法判断改善是否显著。\n→ **需要最少 3 seeds + std 报告。** 无 seed 方差的论文在 CoRL 2025+ 通过率极低。\n\n**W2 [Major]. 单 benchmark 泛化性存疑。**\nSafeLIBERO 基于 LIBERO（tabletop 场景 + MuJoCo），所有碰撞场景都是结构化的 tabletop 环境。无法知道 GeoSafe 是否在 navigation、mobile manipulation、或不同 simulator（ManiSkill、RoboCasa）中同样有效。Title 中 \"Safe Manipulation\" 的 scope 远大于实验覆盖。\n→ 即使不加 benchmark，至少应在 Discussion 中明确界定 claim 范围。\n\n**W3 [Moderate]. 训练时需要 ground-truth SDF labels，实用性受限。**\nSDF labels 来自 MuJoCo 解析几何（mj_geomDistance）。真实场景没有解析几何，论文 Limitations 提到需要 neural SDF estimation，但完全没有实验验证 noisy/approximate SDF labels 下方法是否仍然有效。如果方法对 label quality 极其敏感，实际价值大打折扣。\n→ 建议加一个 SDF label noise robustness 实验（即使在 appendix）。\n\n**W4 [Moderate]. 与 GLaD 的区分不够充分。**\nGLaD (Guo et al., 2025) 同样将几何先验蒸馏到 VLA hidden states，在 LIBERO 上达 94.1% TSR。论文仅说 \"targets general spatial understanding rather than collision safety\"——但审稿人会问：如果 GLaD 的几何蒸馏已经提升了空间理解，那它的碰撞回避是否也间接改善了？缺少与 GLaD 在 SafeLIBERO 上的直接对比。\n→ 至少需要 discussion 段落解释为什么 collision-specific supervision（GeoSafe）优于 general geometric distillation（GLaD）。\n\n**W5 [Minor]. 训练 collision_rate 与 eval CAR 的关系不清。**\n论文 Analysis 段提到 training dynamics，但 collision_rate 是训练期带噪声状态上的 batch metric，与 eval-time 的 CAR（仿真 rollout 中实际碰撞率）可能相关性弱。如果训练 collision_rate 不显著下降但 eval CAR 改善了，机制解释需要加强。\n→ 建议加 training dynamics figure + 明确说明两个 metric 的区别。\n\n---\n\n## 4. Questions for Authors\n\n**Q1.** SDF labels 覆盖 ~75% tasks。缺失 SDF labels 的 25% tasks 在训练时如何处理？这些 tasks 的 eval TSR/CAR 是否与有 SDF 的 tasks 有显著差异？\n\n**Q2.** w(t) 的 t₀=0.5 和 α=10 是如何选择的？是否做过 sensitivity analysis？如果 t₀ 从 0.5 变为 0.3 或 0.7，性能变化多大？（Appendix 有 table 但全是 placeholder）\n\n**Q3.** SDF head 的 262K 参数在 backbone ~300M 参数中占比 <0.09%。训练时 SDF loss 对 backbone 的梯度 magnitude 相对于 flow-matching loss 如何？是否存在梯度冲突？\n\n**Q4.** 论文声称 \"SDF head is discarded after training\"。是否验证过保留 SDF head（verification mode）的 eval 结果与丢弃后一致？如果不一致，说明 SDF prediction 和 action generation 之间存在 decouple。\n\n**Q5.** SafeLIBERO 的 collision 定义是 binary（任何接触 = collision）。对于 soft/partial contact 场景（如推开障碍物才能完成任务），GeoSafe 的 collision penalty 是否会过度抑制必要接触？\n\n---\n\n## 5. Missing Experiments\n\n除 A1/A2/A3 外，审稿人可能要求：\n\n1. **Per-suite breakdown + error bars**（必须）：4 suites × 2 levels 的详细 TSR/CAR/SS，附 std over seeds。当前 Table 1 只有 per-suite avg + overall。\n2. **SDF label noise robustness**（强烈建议）：向 SDF labels 添加 Gaussian noise（σ=1cm, 2cm, 5cm），观察 CAR 退化。直接回应 W3。\n3. **GLaD on SafeLIBERO baseline**（可能被要求）：GLaD 在标准 LIBERO 上 94.1% TSR，但未在 SafeLIBERO 上评估过。如果容易跑，加上。否则至少 discuss。\n4. **Qualitative trajectory visualization**（强烈建议）：展示 vanilla vs GeoSafe 的轨迹差异，直观证明 velocity field 被 shaped。比任何数字都有说服力。\n5. **Training overhead measurement**（easy win）：报告 GeoSafe vs vanilla 的 wall-clock time per step 和 peak memory。当前 Appendix compute cost table 全是 placeholder。\n6. **Learning curve 深度分析**（nice to have）：eval_learning_curve.sh 已就绪。展示 CAR 和 TSR 随 training steps 的变化，说明 safety 什么时候 emerge。\n\n---\n\n## 6. Related Work Gaps\n\n**必须补充：**\n\n- **Safety-Guided Flow (SGF)**（Kim et al., arXiv 2603.13300, March 2026）：统一框架将 CBF-based 安全和 negative guidance 联系起来，**明确分析了 critical time window**——与 GeoSafe 的 w(t) gating 直接相关。不引用此文，了解该领域的审稿人会质疑 novelty。建议在 SDF/Guidance Distillation 段讨论：SGF 提供了理论框架说明 guidance 需要在特定 noise regime 激活，GeoSafe 的 w(t) 可视为此框架在 VLA safety 中的实践。\n\n- **CoFreeVLA**（arxiv 2601.21712, Jan 2026）：dual-arm self-collision avoidance VLA，也使用 risk estimator gate + collision label 训练。虽然 focus 不同（self-collision vs environment collision），但方法论有重叠（training-time collision awareness for VLA）。建议在 Safe Robot Learning 段加一句区分。\n\n**建议补充：**\n\n- **GeoAware-VLA**（arxiv 2509.14117, Sep 2025）：名字和 concept 与 GeoSafe 高度相似——\"Implicit Geometry Aware VLA\"。审稿人搜 related work 时一定会发现。即使 focus 不同也应引用并说明区分。\n\n- **VLA Safety 综述**（arxiv 2604.23775, April 2026）：近期综合 survey，覆盖 training-time 和 inference-time safety。引用可增强 positioning 的完整度。\n\n---\n\n## 7. Overall Assessment\n\n**Score: 5/10**（Borderline — lean reject in current form, lean accept if revisions addressed）\n\n**Confidence: 4/5**\n\n**Justification:**\n\n方法设计优雅（S1-S3），论文写作优秀（S4），\"train-with, infer-without\" 范式有真贡献。但实验覆盖不足以支撑投稿水平的 claim：\n\n- 1 seed 无 error bars（W1）在 CoRL 2025+ 几乎是 auto-reject 信号\n- 单 benchmark（W2）限制了 generalization story\n- SDF label dependency（W3）未被实验验证其鲁棒性边界\n\n**如果修订后达到以下条件，分数可升至 6-7/10（weak accept）：**\n1. ≥3 seeds + std（最关键）\n2. A1+A2+A3 ablation 全部完成\n3. Per-suite breakdown table\n4. 补引 SGF + CoFreeVLA + GeoAware-VLA\n5. Qualitative trajectory visualization\n6. Training overhead 数字填入\n\n**如果 TSR 差距 >5pp**，score 降至 4/10（需要 λ_col sensitivity + tradeoff curve 才能救回）。\n\n---\n\n*注：此评估基于论文框架质量 + 乐观数据假设。实际分数取决于 D005 eval 数字。*",
